        CoredumpFuncMeta,
        Stack,
        executor::handler::state::VmState,
    },
    store::StoreInner,
};
use alloc::{boxed::Box, string::String, vec, vec::Vec};
use core::cell::RefCell;

/// A stack frame captured for a Wasm coredump.
pub(crate) struct CapturedFrame {
    /// The pointer of the [`InstanceEntity`] of the frame.
    pub instance_ptr: usize,
    /// The Wasm function index of the frame within its module.
    pub func_idx: u32,
    /// The local values of the frame.
    pub locals: Vec<CoredumpValue>,
    /// The operand stack values of the frame.
    pub stack: Vec<CoredumpValue>,
}

/// A single value captured for a Wasm coredump.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub(crate) enum CoredumpValue {
    /// A value that could not be recovered.
    Missing,
    /// An `i32` value.
    I32(i32),
    /// An `i64` value.
    I64(i64),
    /// An `f32` value encoded as its IEEE 754 bits.
    F32(u32),
    /// An `f64` value encoded as its IEEE 754 bits.
    F64(u64),
}

/// A guard registering a [`Stack`] as currently active.
///
/// # Note
///
/// This is required to correctly generate Wasm coredumps for re-entrant
/// Wasm executions where multiple [`Stack`]s may be active at the same time.
pub(crate) struct ActiveStackGuard {
    stack: *mut Stack,
}

impl ActiveStackGuard {
    /// Creates a new [`ActiveStackGuard`] for the given [`Stack`].
    pub(crate) fn new(stack: &mut Stack) -> Self {
        let stack = stack as *mut Stack;
        ACTIVE_STACKS.with(|stacks| stacks.borrow_mut().push(stack));
        Self { stack }
    }
}

impl Drop for ActiveStackGuard {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        ACTIVE_STACKS.with(|stacks| {
            let mut stacks = stacks.borrow_mut();
            let popped = stacks.pop();
            debug_assert_eq!(popped, Some(self.stack));
        });
    }
}

thread_local! {
    /// The stacks of all currently active Wasm executions on this thread.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// The stacks are stored in order from outermost to innermost execution.
    static ACTIVE_STACKS: RefCell<Vec<*mut Stack>> = const { RefCell::new(Vec::new()) };
}

/// Returns the [`Stack`]s of all currently active Wasm executions.
///
/// # Note
///
/// The returned stacks are ordered from outermost to innermost execution.
fn active_stacks() -> Vec<*mut Stack> {
    ACTIVE_STACKS.with(|stacks| stacks.borrow().clone())
}

/// Generates a Wasm coredump for the currently active execution.
///
/// Returns `None` if coredump generation is not possible.
pub(crate) fn generate(state: &mut VmState<'_>) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
    let store = state.store.inner();
    let code = state.code;
    let executable_name = store.engine().config().get_coredump_executable_name().to_owned();
    let funcs = code.collect_coredump_funcs();

    // Capture frames from all active stacks, innermost first.
    let stack_pointers = active_stacks();
    let mut frames = Vec::new();
    for stack_ptr in stack_pointers.iter().rev() {
        // Safety: the active stacks are alive for the entire duration of the
        // innermost execution and never mutated while we generate the coredump.
        let stack = unsafe { &**stack_ptr };
        let mut captured = capture_stack(stack, store, &funcs);
        frames.append(&mut captured);
    }

    // Collect modules, instances, memories and globals from the store.
    let mut modules: Vec<ModuleInfo> = Vec::new();
    let mut instances: Vec<&InstanceEntity> = Vec::new();
    let mut memories: Vec<*const CoreMemory> = Vec::new();
    let mut globals: Vec<*const CoreGlobal> = Vec::new();

    for instance in store.instances() {
        if !instance.is_initialized() {
            continue;
        }
        // Add module.
        if let Some(module) = instance.module() {
            let id = module.unique_id();
            if !modules.iter().any(|m| m.id == id) {
                let name = module
                    .name()
                    .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("module-{}", modules.len()));
                modules.push(ModuleInfo { id, name });
            }
        }
        // Add memories and globals.
        for memory in instance.memories() {
            let core = store.resolve_memory(memory);
            let ptr = core as *const CoreMemory;
            if !memories.contains(&ptr) {
                memories.push(ptr);
            }
        }
        for global in instance.globals() {
            let core = store.resolve_global(global);
            if !core.ty().content().is_num() {
                continue;
            }
            let ptr = core as *const CoreGlobal;
            if !globals.contains(&ptr) {
                globals.push(ptr);
            }
        }
        instances.push(instance);
    }

    // Build maps from entity pointer to coredump index.
    let module_index = |instance: &InstanceEntity| -> u32 {
        let Some(module) = instance.module() else {
            return 0;
        };
        let id = module.unique_id();
        modules
            .iter()
            .position(|m| m.id == id)
            .unwrap_or(0) as u32
    };
    let memory_index = |memory: &Memory| -> u32 {
        let core = store.resolve_memory(memory);
        let ptr = core as *const CoreMemory;
        memories
            .iter()
            .position(|m| *m == ptr)
            .unwrap_or(u32::MAX as usize) as u32
    };
    let global_index = |global: &Global| -> Option<u32> {
        let core = store.resolve_global(global);
        if !core.ty().content().is_num() {
            return None;
        }
        let ptr = core as *const CoreGlobal;
        Some(globals.iter().position(|g| *g == ptr).unwrap_or(u32::MAX as usize) as u32)
    };
    let instance_index = |ptr: usize| -> u32 {
        instances
            .iter()
            .position(|i| i as *const InstanceEntity as usize == ptr)
            .unwrap_or(u32::MAX as usize) as u32
    };

    let mut out = Vec::new();

    // 1. "core" custom section.
    let mut core_data = Vec::new();
    core_data.push(0x00);
    push_name(&mut core_data, &executable_name);
    write_custom_section(&mut out, "core", &core_data);

    // 2. Memory section.
    let mut memory_contents = Vec::new();
    {
        let mut contents = Vec::new();
        push_uleb(&mut contents, memories.len() as u64);
        for &memory_ptr in &memories {
            // Safety: all memory pointers refer to live entities in the store.
            let memory = unsafe { &*memory_ptr };
            let ty = memory.dynamic_ty();
            let minimum = ty.minimum();
            let maximum = ty.maximum();
            let mut flags = 0u64;
            if maximum.is_some() {
                flags |= 0x01;
            }
            if ty.is_64() {
                flags |= 0x04;
            }
            push_uleb(&mut contents, flags);
            push_uleb(&mut contents, minimum);
            if let Some(maximum) = maximum {
                push_uleb(&mut contents, maximum);
            }
        }
        write_section(&mut out, 5, &contents);
    }

    // 3. Global section.
    {
        let mut contents = Vec::new();
        push_uleb(&mut contents, globals.len() as u64);
        for &global_ptr in &globals {
            // Safety: all global pointers refer to live entities in the store.
            let global = unsafe { &*global_ptr };
            let ty = global.ty();
            push_val_type(&mut contents, ty.content());
            contents.push(match ty.mutability() {
                crate::core::Mutability::Const => 0x00,
                crate::core::Mutability::Var => 0x01,
            });
            let value = global.get();
            write_const_expr(&mut contents, ty.content(), value.raw().to_bits64());
        }
        write_section(&mut out, 6, &contents);
    }

    // 4. Data section.
    {
        let mut contents = Vec::new();
        push_uleb(&mut contents, memories.len() as u64);
        for (idx, &memory_ptr) in memories.iter().enumerate() {
            // Safety: all memory pointers refer to live entities in the store.
            let memory = unsafe { &*memory_ptr };
            let data = memory.data();
            if idx == 0 {
                contents.push(0x00);
            } else {
                contents.push(0x02);
                push_uleb(&mut contents, idx as u64);
            }
            // i32.const offset 0, end
            contents.push(0x41);
            push_uleb(&mut contents, 0);
            contents.push(0x0B);
            push_uleb(&mut contents, data.len() as u64);
            contents.extend_from_slice(data);
        }
        write_section(&mut out, 11, &contents);
    }

    // 5. "coremodules" custom section.
    let mut modules_data = Vec::new();
    push_uleb(&mut modules_data, modules.len() as u64);
    for module in &modules {
        modules_data.push(0x00);
        push_name(&mut modules_data, &module.name);
    }
    write_custom_section(&mut out, "coremodules", &modules_data);

    // 6. "coreinstances" custom section.
    let mut instances_data = Vec::new();
    push_uleb(&mut instances_data, instances.len() as u64);
    for instance in &instances {
        instances_data.push(0x00);
        push_uleb(&mut instances_data, module_index(instance) as u64);
        let memories = instance
            .memories()
            .iter()
            .map(memory_index)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        push_uleb(&mut instances_data, memories.len() as u64);
        for memory in memories {
            push_uleb(&mut instances_data, memory as u64);
        }
        let globals = instance
            .globals()
            .iter()
            .filter_map(global_index)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        push_uleb(&mut instances_data, globals.len() as u64);
        for global in globals {
            push_uleb(&mut instances_data, global as u64);
        }
    }
    write_custom_section(&mut out, "coreinstances", &instances_data);

    // 7. "corestack" custom section.
    let mut stack_data = Vec::new();
    stack_data.push(0x00);
    push_name(&mut stack_data, "main");
    push_uleb(&mut stack_data, frames.len() as u64);
    for frame in &frames {
        stack_data.push(0x00);
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, instance_index(frame.instance_ptr) as u64);
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, frame.func_idx as u64);
        // Note: Wasmi does not retain enough information to recover the
        //       exact Wasm code offset of the frame.
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, 0);
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, frame.locals.len() as u64);
        for value in &frame.locals {
            write_value(&mut stack_data, *value);
        }
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, frame.stack.len() as u64);
        for value in &frame.stack {
            write_value(&mut stack_data, *value);
        }
    }
    write_custom_section(&mut out, "corestack", &stack_data);

    // Prepend the Wasm header.
    let mut result = Vec::with_capacity(out.len() + 8);
    result.extend_from_slice(b"\0asm");
    result.extend_from_slice(&1u32.to_le_bytes());
    result.extend_from_slice(&out);
    Some(result)
}

/// Information about a module in the coredump.
struct ModuleInfo {
    /// A unique identifier of the module.
    id: *const (),
    /// The name of the module.
    name: String,
}

/// Captures the stack frames of the given [`Stack`].
fn capture_stack(
    stack: &Stack,
    _store: &StoreInner,
    funcs: &[CompiledCoredumpFunc],
) -> Vec<CapturedFrame> {
    let cells = stack.cells();
    let frames = stack.frames();
    let mut result = Vec::new();
    let mut next_start = cells.len();
    let mut cur_instance = stack.current_instance();

    for frame in frames.iter().rev() {
        let start = frame.start_offset();
        let meta = find_func(funcs, frame.ip_ptr());
        let (locals, locals_cells) = match meta {
            Some(meta) => (read_locals(cells, start, &meta.locals), locals_cells(&meta.locals)),
            None => (Vec::new(), 0),
        };
        let locals_end = start.saturating_add(locals_cells);
        let stack_end = next_start.min(cells.len());
        let stack_len = stack_end.saturating_sub(locals_end);
        let stack = (0..stack_len)
            .map(|_| CoredumpValue::Missing)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let instance_ptr = cur_instance
            .map(|inst| inst.as_ptr() as usize)
            .unwrap_or(0);
        result.push(CapturedFrame {
            instance_ptr,
            func_idx: meta.map(|m| m.func_idx).unwrap_or(0),
            locals,
            stack,
        });
        next_start = start;
        // Move to the caller instance.
        if let Some(prev) = frame.instance_opt() {
            cur_instance = Some(prev);
        }
    }
    result
}

/// Finds the coredump metadata of the compiled function containing `ip`.
fn find_func(funcs: &[CompiledCoredumpFunc], ip: *const u8) -> Option<&CoredumpFuncMeta> {
    let addr = ip as usize;
    funcs
        .iter()
        .find(|func| func.start <= addr && addr < func.end)
        .map(|func| &func.meta)
}

/// Returns the number of cells required to store a value of the given type.
fn cells_for_ty(ty: ValType) -> usize {
    if ty == ValType::V128 {
        2
    } else {
        1
    }
}

/// Returns the total number of cells required to store the given local types.
fn locals_cells(locals: &[ValType]) -> usize {
    locals.iter().copied().map(cells_for_ty).sum()
}

/// Reads the local values of a frame from the value stack.
fn read_locals(cells: &[crate::engine::Cell], start: usize, locals: &[ValType]) -> Vec<CoredumpValue> {
    let mut offset = start;
    let mut values = Vec::with_capacity(locals.len());
    for ty in locals {
        let value = match ty {
            ValType::I32 => cells
                .get(offset)
                .map(|cell| CoredumpValue::I32(cell.to_bits() as i32))
                .unwrap_or(CoredumpValue::Missing),
            ValType::I64 => cells
                .get(offset)
                .map(|cell| CoredumpValue::I64(cell.to_bits() as i64))
                .unwrap_or(CoredumpValue::Missing),
            ValType::F32 => cells
                .get(offset)
                .map(|cell| CoredumpValue::F32(cell.to_bits() as u32))
                .unwrap_or(CoredumpValue::Missing),
            ValType::F64 => cells
                .get(offset)
                .map(|cell| CoredumpValue::F64(cell.to_bits()))
                .unwrap_or(CoredumpValue::Missing),
            _ => CoredumpValue::Missing,
        };
        values.push(value);
        offset = offset.saturating_add(cells_for_ty(*ty));
    }
    values
}

/// Writes an unsigned LEB128 encoded `u64` to `out`.
fn push_uleb(out: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: u64) {
    loop {
        let mut byte = (value & 0x7f) as u8;
        value >>= 7;
        if value != 0 {
            byte |= 0x80;
        }
        out.push(byte);
        if value == 0 {
            break;
        }
    }
}

/// Writes a signed LEB128 encoded `i64` to `out`.
fn push_sleb(out: &mut Vec<u8>, value: i64) {
    let mut value = value;
    loop {
        let mut byte = (value & 0x7f) as u8;
        value >>= 7;
        let sign_bit_set = byte & 0x40 != 0;
        let done = (value == 0 && !sign_bit_set) || (value == -1 && sign_bit_set);
        if !done {
            byte |= 0x80;
        }
        out.push(byte);
        if done {
            break;
        }
    }
}

/// Writes a name (LEB128 length-prefixed UTF-8 string) to `out`.
fn push_name(out: &mut Vec<u8>, name: &str) {
    push_uleb(out, name.len() as u64);
    out.extend_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
}

/// Writes a Wasm value type byte to `out`.
fn push_val_type(out: &mut Vec<u8>, ty: ValType) {
    let byte = match ty {
        ValType::I32 => 0x7F,
        ValType::I64 => 0x7E,
        ValType::F32 => 0x7D,
        ValType::F64 => 0x7C,
        ValType::V128 => 0x7B,
        ValType::FuncRef => 0x70,
        ValType::ExternRef => 0x6F,
    };
    out.push(byte);
}

/// Writes a constant expression for the given numeric `value` of type `ty`.
fn write_const_expr(out: &mut Vec<u8>, ty: ValType, bits: u64) {
    match ty {
        ValType::I32 => {
            out.push(0x41);
            push_sleb(out, bits as u32 as i32 as i64);
        }
        ValType::I64 => {
            out.push(0x42);
            push_sleb(out, bits as i64);
        }
        ValType::F32 => {
            out.push(0x43);
            out.extend_from_slice(&(bits as u32).to_le_bytes());
        }
        ValType::F64 => {
            out.push(0x44);
            out.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
        }
        // Note: non-numeric globals are not included in coredumps.
        _ => unreachable!("non-numeric global value in coredump"),
    }
    out.push(0x0B);
}

/// Writes a [`CoredumpValue`] to `out`.
fn write_value(out: &mut Vec<u8>, value: CoredumpValue) {
    match value {
        CoredumpValue::Missing => out.push(0x01),
        CoredumpValue::I32(value) => {
            out.push(0x7F);
            push_sleb(out, value as i64);
        }
        CoredumpValue::I64(value) => {
            out.push(0x7E);
            push_sleb(out, value);
        }
        CoredumpValue::F32(bits) => {
            out.push(0x7D);
            out.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
        }
        CoredumpValue::F64(bits) => {
            out.push(0x7C);
            out.extend_from_slice(&bits.to_le_bytes());
        }
    }
}

/// Writes a custom section with the given `name` and `data` to `out`.
fn write_custom_section(out: &mut Vec<u8>, name: &str, data: &[u8]) {
    let mut payload = Vec::new();
    push_name(&mut payload, name);
    payload.extend_from_slice(data);
    out.push(0x00);
    push_uleb(out, payload.len() as u64);
    out.extend_from_slice(&payload);
}

/// Writes a standard Wasm section with the given `id` and `contents` to `out`.
fn write_section(out: &mut Vec<u8>, id: u8, contents: &[u8]) {
    out.push(id);
    push_uleb(out, contents.len() as u64);
    out.extend_from_slice(contents);
}

returncode=0
        results: Results,
    ) -> Result<Results::Value, Error>
    where
        Params: LowerToCells,
        Results: LiftFromCells,
    {
        let mut stack = self.stacks.lock().reuse_or_new();
        let value = EngineExecutor::new(&self.code_map, &mut stack)
            .execute_root_func(ctx.store, func, params, results)
            .map_err(ExecutionOutcome::into_non_resumable)?;
        self.stacks.lock().recycle(stack);
        Ok(value)
    }

    /// Executes the given [`Func`] resumably with the given `params` and returns the `results`.
    ///
    /// Uses the [`StoreContextMut`] for context information about the Wasm [`Store`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the Wasm execution traps or runs out of resources.
    pub fn execute_func_resumable<T, Params, Results>(
        &self,
        ctx: StoreContextMut<T>,
        func: &Func,
        params: Params,
        results: Results,
    ) -> Result<ResumableCallBase<Results::Value>, Error>
    where
        Params: LowerToCells,
        Results: LiftFromCells,
    {
        let store = ctx.store;
        let mut stack = self.stacks.lock().reuse_or_new();
        let outcome = EngineExecutor::new(&self.code_map, &mut stack)
            .execute_root_func(store, func, params, results);
        let value = match outcome {
            Ok(value) => value,
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::Host(error)) => {
                let host_func = *error.host_func();
                let caller_results = *error.caller_results();
                let host_error = error.into_error();
                return Ok(ResumableCallBase::HostTrap(ResumableCallHostTrap::new(
                    store.engine().clone(),
                    stack,
                    *func,
                    host_func,
                    host_error,
                    caller_results,
                )));
            }
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::OutOfFuel(error)) => {
                let required_fuel = error.required_fuel();
                return Ok(ResumableCallBase::OutOfFuel(ResumableCallOutOfFuel::new(
                    store.engine().clone(),
                    stack,
                    *func,
                    required_fuel,
                )));
            }
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::Error(error)) => {
                self.stacks.lock().recycle(stack);
                return Err(error);
            }
        };
        self.stacks.lock().recycle(stack);
    ///
    /// Uses the [`StoreContextMut`] for context information about the Wasm [`Store`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the Wasm execution traps or runs out of resources.
    pub fn resume_func_host_trap<T, Params, Results>(
        &self,
        ctx: StoreContextMut<T>,
        mut invocation: ResumableCallHostTrap,
        params: Params,
        results: Results,
    ) -> Result<ResumableCallBase<Results::Value>, Error>
    where
        Params: LowerToCells,
        Results: LiftFromCells,
    {
        let caller_results = invocation.caller_results();
        let mut executor = EngineExecutor::new(&self.code_map, invocation.common.stack_mut());
        let outcome = executor.resume_func_host_trap(ctx.store, params, caller_results, results);
        let results = match outcome {
            Ok(results) => results,
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::Host(error)) => {
                let host_func = *error.host_func();
                let caller_results = *error.caller_results();
                invocation.update(host_func, error.into_error(), caller_results);
                return Ok(ResumableCallBase::HostTrap(invocation));
            }
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::OutOfFuel(error)) => {
                let required_fuel = error.required_fuel();
                let invocation = invocation.update_to_out_of_fuel(required_fuel);
                return Ok(ResumableCallBase::OutOfFuel(invocation));
            }
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::Error(error)) => {
                self.stacks.lock().recycle(invocation.common.take_stack());
                return Err(error);
            }
        };
        self.stacks.lock().recycle(invocation.common.take_stack());
        Ok(ResumableCallBase::Finished(results))
    }

    /// Resumes the given [`Func`] after running out of fuel and returns the `results`.
    ///
    /// Uses the [`StoreContextMut`] for context information about the Wasm [`Store`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the Wasm execution traps or runs out of resources.
    pub fn resume_func_out_of_fuel<T, Results>(
        &self,
        ctx: StoreContextMut<T>,
        mut invocation: ResumableCallOutOfFuel,
        results: Results,
    ) -> Result<ResumableCallBase<Results::Value>, Error>
    where
        Results: LiftFromCells,
    {
        let mut executor = EngineExecutor::new(&self.code_map, invocation.common.stack_mut());
        let outcome = executor.resume_func_out_of_fuel(ctx.store, results);
        let results = match outcome {
            Ok(results) => results,
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::Host(error)) => {
                let host_func = *error.host_func();
                let caller_results = *error.caller_results();
                let invocation =
                    invocation.update_to_host_trap(host_func, error.into_error(), caller_results);
                return Ok(ResumableCallBase::HostTrap(invocation));
            }
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::OutOfFuel(error)) => {
                invocation.update(error.required_fuel());
                return Ok(ResumableCallBase::OutOfFuel(invocation));
            }
            Err(ExecutionOutcome::Error(error)) => {
                self.stacks.lock().recycle(invocation.common.take_stack());
                return Err(error);
            }
        };
        self.stacks.lock().recycle(invocation.common.take_stack());
        Ok(ResumableCallBase::Finished(results))
    }

END STEP

old = """/// A guard registering a [`Stack`] as currently active.
///
/// # Note
///
/// This is required to correctly generate Wasm coredumps for re-entrant
/// Wasm executions where multiple [`Stack`]s may be active at the same time.
pub(crate) struct ActiveStackGuard {
    stack: *mut Stack,
}

impl ActiveStackGuard {
    /// Creates a new [`ActiveStackGuard`] for the given [`Stack`].
    pub(crate) fn new(stack: &mut Stack) -> Self {
        let stack = stack as *mut Stack;
        ACTIVE_STACKS.with(|stacks| stacks.borrow_mut().push(stack));
        Self { stack }
    }
}

impl Drop for ActiveStackGuard {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        ACTIVE_STACKS.with(|stacks| {
            let mut stacks = stacks.borrow_mut();
            let popped = stacks.pop();
            debug_assert_eq!(popped, Some(self.stack));
        });
    }
}

thread_local! {
    /// The stacks of all currently active Wasm executions on this thread.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// The stacks are stored in order from outermost to innermost execution.
    static ACTIVE_STACKS: RefCell<Vec<*mut Stack>> = const { RefCell::new(Vec::new()) };
}

/// Returns the [`Stack`]s of all currently active Wasm executions.
///
/// # Note
///
/// The returned stacks are ordered from outermost to innermost execution.
fn active_stacks() -> Vec<*mut Stack> {
    ACTIVE_STACKS.with(|stacks| stacks.borrow().clone())
}
"""
new = """/// A guard registering a [`Stack`] as currently active.
///
/// # Note
///
/// This is required to correctly generate Wasm coredumps for re-entrant
/// Wasm executions where multiple [`Stack`]s may be active at the same time.
pub(crate) struct ActiveStackGuard {
    stack: *mut Stack,
    store: *mut StoreInner,
}

impl ActiveStackGuard {
    /// Creates a new [`ActiveStackGuard`] for the given [`Stack`] and [`StoreInner`].
    pub(crate) fn new(stack: &mut Stack, store: &mut StoreInner) -> Self {
        let stack = stack as *mut Stack;
        let store = store as *mut StoreInner;
        // Safety: the store is alive for the entire duration of the guard.
        unsafe { (&mut *store).push_active_stack(stack) };
        Self { stack, store }
    }
}

impl Drop for ActiveStackGuard {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        // Safety: the store is alive for the entire duration of the guard.
        unsafe { (&mut *self.store).pop_active_stack(self.stack) };
    }
}
"""
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old, new)

# Replace active_stacks usage in generate
s=s.replace("""    let funcs = code.collect_coredump_funcs();

    // Capture frames from all active stacks, innermost first.
    let stack_pointers = active_stacks();
    let mut frames = Vec::new();
    for stack_ptr in stack_pointers.iter().rev() {
        // Safety: the active stacks are alive for the entire duration of the
        // innermost execution and never mutated while we generate the coredump.
        let stack = unsafe { &**stack_ptr };
        let mut captured = capture_stack(stack, store, &funcs);
        frames.append(&mut captured);
    }""",

use crate::{Engine, Error, Store};

/// A trait used to get shared access to a [`Store`] in Wasmi.
pub trait AsContext {
    /// The user state associated with the [`Store`], aka the `T` in `Store<T>`.
    type Data;

    /// Returns the store context that this type provides access to.
    fn as_context(&self) -> StoreContext<'_, Self::Data>;
}

/// A trait used to get exclusive access to a [`Store`] in Wasmi.
pub trait AsContextMut: AsContext {
    /// Returns the store context that this type provides access to.
    fn as_context_mut(&mut self) -> StoreContextMut<'_, Self::Data>;
}

/// A temporary handle to a [`&Store<T>`][`Store`].
///
/// This type is suitable for [`AsContext`] trait bounds on methods if desired.
/// For more information, see [`Store`].
#[derive(Debug)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct StoreContext<'a, T> {
    pub(crate) store: &'a Store<T>,
}

impl<T> Clone for StoreContext<'_, T> {
    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
        *self
    }
}

impl<T> Copy for StoreContext<'_, T> {}

impl<T> StoreContext<'_, T> {
    /// Returns the underlying [`Engine`] this store is connected to.
    pub fn engine(&self) -> &Engine {
        self.store.engine()
    }

    /// Access the underlying data owned by this store.
    ///
    /// Same as [`Store::data`].
    pub fn data(&self) -> &T {
        self.store.data()
    }

    /// Returns the remaining fuel of the [`Store`] if fuel metering is enabled.

    /// For more information see [`Store::get_fuel`](crate::Store::get_fuel).
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If fuel metering is disabled.
    pub fn get_fuel(&self) -> Result<u64, Error> {
        self.store.get_fuel()
    }
}

            | Self::Table(TableError::FillOutOfBounds)
            | Self::Table(TableError::GrowOutOfBounds)
            | Self::Table(TableError::InitOutOfBounds) => TrapCode::TableOutOfBounds,
            _ => return None,

    }

returncode=0

impl<I> ops::Index<I> for SmallByteSlice
where
    I: slice::SliceIndex<[u8]>,
{
    type Output = <I as slice::SliceIndex<[u8]>>::Output;

    #[inline]
    fn index(&self, index: I) -> &Self::Output {
        self.as_slice().index(index)
    }
}

impl<'a> From<&'a [u8]> for SmallByteSlice {
    fn from(bytes: &'a [u8]) -> Self {
        if bytes.len() <= Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE {
            let len = bytes.len() as u8;
            let mut buffer = [0x00_u8; Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE];
            buffer[..usize::from(len)].copy_from_slice(bytes);
            return Self::Small { len, bytes: buffer };
        }
        Self::Big(bytes.into())
    }
}

/// Meta information about a [`EngineFunc`].
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CompiledFuncEntity {
    /// The sequence of [`Op`] of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
    ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>>,
    /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This includes stack slots to store the function local constant values,
    /// function parameters, function locals and dynamically used stack slots.
    len_stack_slots: u16,
}

impl CompiledFuncEntity {
    /// Returns the sequence of encoded [`Op`]s of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
    pub(crate) fn ops(&self) -> &[u8] {
        self.ops.as_ref()
    }

    /// Returns the coredump metadata of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`] if any.
    pub(crate) fn coredump_meta(&self) -> Option<&CoredumpFuncMeta> {
        self.coredump.as_ref()
    }

    /// Create a new initialized [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// - If `ops` is empty.
    /// - If `ops` contains more than `i32::MAX` encoded bytes.
    pub fn new(len_stack_slots: u16, ops: &[u8], coredump: Option<CoredumpFuncMeta>) -> Self {
        let ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>> = Pin::new(ops.into());
        assert!(
            !ops.is_empty(),
            "compiled functions must have at least one instruction"
        );
        assert!(
            // Generally, Wasmi has no issues with more than `i32::MAX` instructions.
            // However, Wasmi's branch instructions can jump across at most `i32::MAX`
            // forwards or `i32::MIN` instructions backwards and thus having more than
            // `i32::MAX` instructions might introduce problems.
            ops.len() <= i32::MAX as usize,
            "compiled function has too many instructions: {}",
            ops.len(),
        );
        Self {
            ops,
            len_stack_slots,
            coredump,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]

END STEP

use wasmi::{Config, Engine, Linker, Module, Store};

#[test]
fn coredump_generated_on_trap() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    config.coredump_executable_name("myexe");
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (func (export "trap") (param i32) (result i32)
                local.get 0
                local.get 0
                i32.add
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<i32, i32>(&store, "trap").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, 5).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("coredump should be present");
    assert_eq!(&coredump[..4], b"\0asm");
    assert_eq!(&coredump[4..8], &1u32.to_le_bytes());
}

#[test]
fn no_coredump_without_enabling() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let module = Module::new(&engine, r#"(module (func (export "trap") unreachable))"#).unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "trap").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    assert!(error.coredump().is_none());
}
EOF

cd /app && cat > /tmp/parse_test.rs <<'EOF'
use wasmi::{Config, Engine, Linker, Module, Store};
use wasmparser::{Parser, Payload, CoreDumpStackSection, CoreDumpInstancesSection, CoreDumpModulesSection, CoreDumpSection};

#[test]
fn coredump_sections_and_frames() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    config.coredump_executable_name("myexe");
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (memory (export "mem") 1)
            (global (export "g") i32 (i32.const 7))
            (func (export "trap") (param i32) (param i64) (param f32) (param f64) (local i32)
                local.get 0
                drop
                local.get 1
                drop
                local.get 2
                drop
                local.get 3
                drop
                i32.const 99
                local.set 4
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(i32, i64, f32, f64), ()>(&store, "trap").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, (42, -7, 1.5f32, -2.25f64)).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("coredump should be present");
    std::fs::write("/tmp/coredump.wasm", coredump).unwrap();

    let mut saw_core = false;
    let mut saw_modules = false;
    let mut saw_instances = false;
    let mut saw_stack = false;
    let mut saw_memory = false;
    let mut saw_global = false;
    let mut saw_data = false;

    for payload in Parser::new(0).parse_all(coredump) {
        let payload = payload.unwrap();
        match payload {
            Payload::CustomSection(reader) => match reader.name() {
                "core" => {
                    saw_core = true;
                    let data = reader.data();
                    let sec = CoreDumpSection::new(wasmparser::BinaryReader::new(data, 0)).unwrap();
                    assert_eq!(sec.name, "myexe");
                }
                "coremodules" => {
                    saw_modules = true;
                    let data = reader.data();
                    let sec = CoreDumpModulesSection::new(wasmparser::BinaryReader::new(data, 0)).unwrap();
                    eprintln!("modules: {:?}", sec.modules);
                }
                "coreinstances" => {
                    saw_instances = true;
                    let data = reader.data();
                    let sec = CoreDumpInstancesSection::new(wasmparser::BinaryReader::new(data, 0)).unwrap();
                    eprintln!("instances: {:?}", sec.instances);
                }
                "corestack" => {
                    saw_stack = true;
                    let data = reader.data();
                    let sec = CoreDumpStackSection::new(wasmparser::BinaryReader::new(data, 0)).unwrap();
                    eprintln!("name: {:?} frames: {}", sec.name, sec.frames.len());
                    assert_eq!(sec.name, "main");
                    assert_eq!(sec.frames.len(), 1);
                    let frame = &sec.frames[0];
                    assert_eq!(frame.funcidx, 0);
                    assert_eq!(frame.codeoffset, 0);
                    eprintln!("locals: {:?}", frame.locals);
                    assert_eq!(frame.locals.len(), 5);
                    match frame.locals[0] { wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::I32(v) => assert_eq!(v, 42), _ => panic!() }
                    match frame.locals[1] { wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::I64(v) => assert_eq!(v, -7), _ => panic!() }
                    match frame.locals[2] { wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::F32(v) => assert_eq!(v, 1.5), _ => panic!() }
                    match frame.locals[3] { wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::F64(v) => assert_eq!(v, -2.25), _ => panic!() }
                    match frame.locals[4] { wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::I32(v) => assert_eq!(v, 99), _ => panic!() }
                }
                _ => {}
            },
            Payload::MemorySection(_) => saw_memory = true,
            Payload::GlobalSection(_) => saw_global = true,
            Payload::DataSection(_) => saw_data = true,
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    assert!(saw_core);
    assert!(saw_modules);
    assert!(saw_instances);
    assert!(saw_stack);
    assert!(saw_memory);
    assert!(saw_global);
    assert!(saw_data);
}
EOF

COMMAND:
cd /app && cat > crates/wasmi/tests/parse_coredump.rs <<'EOF'
use wasmi::{Config, Engine, Linker, Module, Store};
use wasmparser::{
    BinaryReader, CoreDumpInstancesSection, CoreDumpModulesSection, CoreDumpSection,
    CoreDumpStackSection, DataKind, GlobalType, Parser, Payload,
};

#[test]
fn coredump_sections_and_frames() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    config.coredump_executable_name("myexe");
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (memory (export "mem") 1)
            (global (export "g") i32 (i32.const 7))
            (func (export "trap") (param i32) (param i64) (param f32) (param f64) (local i32)
                local.get 0
                drop
                local.get 1
                drop
                local.get 2
                drop
                local.get 3
                drop
                i32.const 99
                local.set 4
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(i32, i64, f32, f64), ()>(&store, "trap").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, (42, -7, 1.5f32, -2.25f64)).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("coredump should be present");

    let mut saw_core = false;
    let mut saw_modules = false;
    let mut saw_instances = false;
    let mut saw_stack = false;
    let mut saw_memory = false;
    let mut saw_global = false;
    let mut saw_data = false;

    for payload in Parser::new(0).parse_all(coredump) {
        let payload = payload.unwrap();
        match payload {
            Payload::CustomSection(reader) => match reader.name() {
                "core" => {
                    saw_core = true;
                    let data = reader.data();
                    let sec = CoreDumpSection::new(BinaryReader::new(data, 0)).unwrap();
                    assert_eq!(sec.name, "myexe");
                }
                "coremodules" => {
                    saw_modules = true;
                    let data = reader.data();
                    let sec = CoreDumpModulesSection::new(BinaryReader::new(data, 0)).unwrap();
                    assert_eq!(sec.modules.len(), 1);
                }
                "coreinstances" => {
                    saw_instances = true;
                    let data = reader.data();
                    let sec = CoreDumpInstancesSection::new(BinaryReader::new(data, 0)).unwrap();
                    assert_eq!(sec.instances.len(), 1);
                    let instance = &sec.instances[0];
                    assert_eq!(instance.module_index, 0);
                    assert_eq!(instance.memories, vec![0]);
                    assert_eq!(instance.globals, vec![0]);
                }
                "corestack" => {
                    saw_stack = true;
                    let data = reader.data();
                    let sec = CoreDumpStackSection::new(BinaryReader::new(data, 0)).unwrap();
                    assert_eq!(sec.name, "main");
                    assert_eq!(sec.frames.len(), 1);
                    let frame = &sec.frames[0];
                    assert_eq!(frame.instanceidx, 0);
                    assert_eq!(frame.funcidx, 0);
                    assert_eq!(frame.codeoffset, 0);
                    assert_eq!(frame.locals.len(), 5);
                    match frame.locals[0] {
                        wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::I32(v) => assert_eq!(v, 42),
                        _ => panic!("expected i32 local"),
                    }
                    match frame.locals[1] {
                        wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::I64(v) => assert_eq!(v, -7),
                        _ => panic!("expected i64 local"),
                    }
                    match frame.locals[2] {
                        wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::F32(v) => assert_eq!(v, 1.5),
                        _ => panic!("expected f32 local"),
                    }
                    match frame.locals[3] {
                        wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::F64(v) => assert_eq!(v, -2.25),
                        _ => panic!("expected f64 local"),
                    }
                    match frame.locals[4] {
                        wasmparser::CoreDumpValue::I32(v) => assert_eq!(v, 99),
                        _ => panic!("expected local i32"),
                    }
                }
                _ => {}
            },
            Payload::MemorySection(reader) => {
                saw_memory = true;
                let mems: Vec<_> = reader.into_iter().collect::<Result<_, _>>().unwrap();
                assert_eq!(mems.len(), 1);
                let ty = mems[0];
                assert_eq!(ty.minimum, 1);
                assert_eq!(ty.maximum, None);
                assert!(!ty.memory64);
            }
            Payload::GlobalSection(reader) => {
                saw_global = true;
                let globals: Vec<_> = reader
                    .into_iter()
                    .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()
                    .unwrap();
                assert_eq!(globals.len(), 1);
                let global = &globals[0];
                assert_eq!(global.ty, GlobalType {
                    val_type: wasmparser::ValType::I32,
                    mutable: false,
                    shared: false,
                });
            }
            Payload::DataSection(reader) => {
                saw_data = true;
                let data: Vec<_> = reader.into_iter().collect::<Result<_, _>>().unwrap();
                assert_eq!(data.len(), 1);
                match &data[0].kind {
                    DataKind::Active {
                        memory_index,
                        init_expr,
                    } => {
                        assert_eq!(*memory_index, 0);
                        let mut ops = init_expr.get_operators_reader();
                        let op = ops.read().unwrap();
                        match op {
                            wasmparser::Operator::I32Const { value } => assert_eq!(value, 0),
                            _ => panic!("expected i32.const 0 offset"),
                        }
                        assert_eq!(data[0].data.len(), 65536);
                    }
                    _ => panic!("expected active data segment"),
                }
            }
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    assert!(saw_core);
    assert!(saw_modules);
    assert!(saw_instances);
    assert!(saw_stack);
    assert!(saw_memory);
    assert!(saw_global);
    assert!(saw_data);
}
EOF

STEP 160 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && cat > crates/wasmi/tests/coredump_reentrant.rs <<'EOF'
use wasmi::{BinaryReader, Caller, Config, CoreDumpStackSection, Engine, Linker, Module, Store};

#[test]
fn coredump_includes_outer_frames_on_reentrancy() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);

    // Inner module: traps when called.
    let inner_module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (func (export "inner") (param i32)
                local.get 0
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let inner_linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let inner_instance = inner_linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &inner_module)
        .unwrap();
    let inner = inner_instance
        .get_typed_func::<i32, ()>(&store, "inner")
        .unwrap();

    // Outer module: calls a host function which re-enters Wasm.
    let outer_module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (import "host" "callback" (func $callback (param i32)))
            (func (export "outer") (param i32)
                local.get 0
                call $callback
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();

    let mut linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let inner = std::sync::Arc::new(inner);
    let inner2 = inner.clone();
    linker.func_wrap("host", "callback", move |caller: Caller<'_, ()>, value: i32| {
        let inner = inner2.clone();
        inner.call(caller, value).map(|_| ())
    });
    let outer_instance = linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &outer_module)
        .unwrap();
    let outer = outer_instance
        .get_typed_func::<i32, ()>(&store, "outer")
        .unwrap();

    let error = outer.call(&mut store, 5).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("coredump should be present");
    let mut stack = None;
    for payload in wasmparser::Parser::new(0).parse_all(coredump) {
        let payload = payload.unwrap();
        if let wasmparser::Payload::CustomSection(reader) = payload {
            if reader.name() == "corestack" {
                stack = Some(
                    CoreDumpStackSection::new(BinaryReader::new(reader.data(), 0)).unwrap(),
                );
            }
        }
    }
    let stack = stack.expect("corestack section");
    assert_eq!(stack.frames.len(), 2, "expected inner and outer frames");
    // Youngest (inner) frame first, then outer frame.
    assert_ne!(
        stack.frames[0].instanceidx, stack.frames[1].instanceidx,
        "inner and outer frames should belong to different instances"
    );
    assert_eq!(stack.frames[0].funcidx, 0);
    assert_eq!(stack.frames[1].funcidx, 0);
}
EOF

252:    pub fn as_trap_code(&self) -> Option<TrapCode> {
254:            | Self::TrapCode(trap_code)
255:            | Self::TrapCodeWithCoredump(trap_code, _) => *trap_code,
259:            | Self::Memory(MemoryError::OutOfFuel { .. }) => TrapCode::OutOfFuel,
261:            | Self::Memory(MemoryError::OutOfBoundsGrowth) => TrapCode::MemoryOutOfBounds,
262:            | Self::Table(TableError::ElementTypeMismatch) => TrapCode::BadSignature,
266:            | Self::Table(TableError::InitOutOfBounds) => TrapCode::TableOutOfBounds,

350:    fn from(error: TrapCode) -> Self {

    Wat(WatError),
}

impl ErrorKind {
    /// Returns a reference to [`TrapCode`] if [`ErrorKind`] is a [`TrapCode`].
    /// Returns the generated Wasm coredump bytes of the [`ErrorKind`] if any.
    pub fn as_coredump(&self) -> Option<&[u8]> {
        match self {
            Self::TrapCodeWithCoredump(_, coredump) => coredump.as_deref(),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Returns a reference to [`TrapCode`] if [`ErrorKind`] is a [`TrapCode`].
    pub fn as_trap_code(&self) -> Option<TrapCode> {
        let trap_code = match self {
            | Self::TrapCode(trap_code)
            | Self::TrapCodeWithCoredump(trap_code, _) => *trap_code,
            | Self::ResumableOutOfFuel(_)
            | Self::Fuel(FuelError::OutOfFuel { .. })
            | Self::Table(TableError::OutOfFuel { .. })
            | Self::Memory(MemoryError::OutOfFuel { .. }) => TrapCode::OutOfFuel,
            | Self::Memory(MemoryError::OutOfBoundsAccess)

END STEP

use wasmi::{Config, Engine, Linker, Module, Store};
use wasmparser::{BinaryReader, CoreDumpModulesSection, DataKind, GlobalType, Parser, Payload};

#[test]
fn coredump_module_name_memory_and_globals() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module $my_module
            (memory (export "mem") 1)
            (global (export "const_g") i32 (i32.const 7))
            (global (export "mut_g") (mut f64) (f64.const 1.5))
            (func (export "setup_and_trap")
                i32.const 0
                i32.const 1234
                i32.store
                global.set 1
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "setup_and_trap").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("coredump");

    let mut module_names = None;
    let mut global_section = None;
    let mut data_section = None;
    for payload in Parser::new(0).parse_all(coredump) {
        let payload = payload.unwrap();
        match payload {
            Payload::CustomSection(reader) if reader.name() == "coremodules" => {
                module_names = Some(
                    CoreDumpModulesSection::new(BinaryReader::new(reader.data(), 0)).unwrap(),
                );
            }
            Payload::GlobalSection(reader) => {
                global_section = Some(
                    reader
                        .into_iter()
                        .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()
                        .unwrap(),
                );
            }
            Payload::DataSection(reader) => {
                data_section = Some(reader.into_iter().collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>().unwrap());
            }
            _ => {}
        }
    }

    let module_names = module_names.expect("coremodules");
    assert_eq!(module_names.modules, vec!["my_module"]);

    let globals = global_section.expect("global section");
    assert_eq!(globals.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(
        globals[0].ty,
        GlobalType { content_type: wasmparser::ValType::I32, mutable: false, shared: false }
    );
    assert_eq!(
        globals[1].ty,
        GlobalType { content_type: wasmparser::ValType::F64, mutable: true, shared: false }
    );

    let data = data_section.expect("data section");
    assert_eq!(data.len(), 1);
    match &data[0].kind {
        DataKind::Active { memory_index, offset_expr } => {
            assert_eq!(*memory_index, 0);
            let mut ops = offset_expr.get_operators_reader();
            match ops.read().unwrap() {
                wasmparser::Operator::I32Const { value } => assert_eq!(value, 0),
                _ => panic!("expected i32.const"),
            }
        }
        _ => panic!("expected active data segment"),
    }
    // Check that the bytes 1234 (0xD2 0x04) were written to memory at offset 0.
    assert_eq!(data[0].data[0], 0xD2);
    assert_eq!(data[0].data[1], 0x04);
}
EOF

use crate::{BinaryReader, ConstExpr, FromReader, GlobalType, Result, SectionLimited};

/// Represents a core WebAssembly global.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Global<'a> {
    /// The global's type.
    pub ty: GlobalType,
    /// The global's initialization expression.
    pub init_expr: ConstExpr<'a>,
}

/// A reader for the global section of a WebAssembly module.
pub type GlobalSectionReader<'a> = SectionLimited<'a, Global<'a>>;

impl<'a> FromReader<'a> for Global<'a> {
    fn from_reader(reader: &mut BinaryReader<'a>) -> Result<Self> {
        let ty = reader.read()?;
        let init_expr = reader.read()?;
        Ok(Global { ty, init_expr })
    }
}

impl<'a> FromReader<'a> for GlobalType {
    fn from_reader(reader: &mut BinaryReader<'a>) -> Result<Self> {
        let content_type = reader.read()?;
        let flags = reader.read_u8()?;
        if reader.shared_everything_threads() {
            if flags > 0b11 {
                bail!(reader.original_position() - 1, "malformed global flags")
            }
        } else {
            if flags > 0b1 {
                bail!(
                    reader.original_position() - 1,
                    "malformed mutability -- or shared globals \

END STEP

COMMAND:
cd /app && cat > crates/wasmi/tests/coredump_edge.rs <<'EOF'
use wasmi::{BinaryReader, Config, CoreDumpStackSection, Engine, Linker, Module, Parser, Payload, Store};

fn parse_frames(coredump: &[u8]) -> Vec<(u32, u32)> {
    let mut frames = Vec::new();
    for payload in Parser::new(0).parse_all(coredump) {
        let payload = payload.unwrap();
        if let Payload::CustomSection(reader) = payload {
            if reader.name() == "corestack" {
                let sec = CoreDumpStackSection::new(BinaryReader::new(reader.data(), 0)).unwrap();
                frames = sec.frames.iter().map(|f| (f.instanceidx, f.funcidx)).collect();
            }
        }
    }
    frames
}

#[test]
fn coredump_on_start_function_trap() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (func $start
                unreachable
            )
            (start $start)
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let error = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("coredump should be present");
    let frames = parse_frames(coredump);
    assert_eq!(frames.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(frames[0].1, 0);
}

#[test]
fn coredump_recursion_has_all_frames() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (func $rec (export "rec") (param i32)
                local.get 0
                i32.const 1
                i32.sub
                local.tee 0
                if
                    local.get 0
                    call $rec
                else
                    unreachable
                end
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<i32, ()>(&store, "rec").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, 3).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("coredump should be present");
    let frames = parse_frames(coredump);
    // Recurses 3 -> 2 -> 1 -> 0 traps, so 4 frames.
    assert_eq!(frames.len(), 4);
    // All frames belong to the same instance and function.
    assert!(frames.iter().all(|(i, f)| *i == frames[0].0 && *f == 0));
}
EOF

OBSERVATION:
returncode=0
use crate::arena::ArenaKey;
use alloc::vec::Vec;
use core::{
    fmt::{self, Debug},
    marker::PhantomData,
    ops::{Index, IndexMut},
};

/// Stores components for entities backed by a [`Vec`].
pub struct ComponentVec<Key, T> {
    components: Vec<Option<T>>,
    marker: PhantomData<fn() -> Key>,
}

/// [`ComponentVec`] does not store `Key` therefore it is `Send` without its bound.
unsafe impl<Key, T> Send for ComponentVec<Key, T> where T: Send {}

/// [`ComponentVec`] does not store `Key` therefore it is `Sync` without its bound.
unsafe impl<Key, T> Sync for ComponentVec<Key, T> where T: Sync {}

impl<Key, T> Debug for ComponentVec<Key, T>
where
    T: Debug,
{
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("ComponentVec")
            .field("components", &DebugComponents(&self.components))
            .finish()
    }
}

struct DebugComponents<'a, T>(&'a [Option<T>]);

impl<T> Debug for DebugComponents<'_, T>
where
    T: Debug,
{
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        let mut map = f.debug_map();
        let components = self
            .0
            .iter()
            .enumerate()
            .filter_map(|(n, component)| component.as_ref().map(|c| (n, c)));
        for (key, component) in components {
            map.entry(&key, component);
        }
        map.finish()
    }
}

impl<Key, T> Default for ComponentVec<Key, T> {
    #[inline]
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl<Key, T> PartialEq for ComponentVec<Key, T>
where
    T: PartialEq,
{
    #[inline]
    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        self.components.eq(&other.components)
    }
}

impl<Key, T> Eq for ComponentVec<Key, T> where T: Eq {}

impl<Key, T> ComponentVec<Key, T> {
    /// Creates a new empty [`ComponentVec`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            components: Vec::new(),
            marker: PhantomData,
        }
    }

    /// Clears all components from the [`ComponentVec`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn clear(&mut self) {
        self.components.clear();
    }
}

impl<Key, T> ComponentVec<Key, T>
where
    Key: ArenaKey,
{
    /// Sets the `component` for the entity at `index`.
    ///
    /// Returns the old component of the same entity if any.
    pub fn set(&mut self, index: Key, component: T) -> Option<T> {
        let index = index.into_usize();
        if index >= self.components.len() {
            // The underlying vector does not have enough capacity
            // and is required to be enlarged.
            self.components.resize_with(index + 1, || None);
        }
        self.components[index].replace(component)
    }

    /// Unsets the component for the entity at `index` and returns it if any.
    #[inline]
    pub fn unset(&mut self, index: Key) -> Option<T> {
        self.components
            .get_mut(index.into_usize())
            .and_then(Option::take)
    }

    /// Returns a shared reference to the component at the `index` if any.
    ///
    /// Returns `None` if no component is stored under the `index`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn get(&self, index: Key) -> Option<&T> {
        self.components
            .get(index.into_usize())
            .and_then(Option::as_ref)
    }

    /// Returns an exclusive reference to the component at the `index` if any.
    ///
    /// Returns `None` if no component is stored under the `index`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn get_mut(&mut self, index: Key) -> Option<&mut T> {
        self.components
            .get_mut(index.into_usize())
            .and_then(Option::as_mut)
    }
}

impl<Key, T> Index<Key> for ComponentVec<Key, T>
where
    Key: ArenaKey,
{
    type Output = T;

    #[inline]
    fn index(&self, index: Key) -> &Self::Output {
        self.get(index)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing component at index: {}", index.into_usize()))
    }
}

impl<Key, T> IndexMut<Key> for ComponentVec<Key, T>
where
    Key: ArenaKey,
{
    #[inline]
    fn index_mut(&mut self, index: Key) -> &mut Self::Output {
        self.get_mut(index)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing component at index: {}", index.into_usize()))
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::{format, string::String};

    /// Add `n` components and perform checks along the way.
    fn add_components(vec: &mut ComponentVec<usize, String>, n: usize) {
        for i in 0..n {
            let mut str = format!("{i}");
            assert!(vec.get(i).is_none());
            assert!(vec.get_mut(i).is_none());
            assert!(vec.set(i, str.clone()).is_none());
            assert_eq!(vec.get(i), Some(&str));
            assert_eq!(vec.get_mut(i), Some(&mut str));
            assert_eq!(&vec[i], &str);
            assert_eq!(&mut vec[i], &mut str);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn it_works() {
        let mut vec = <ComponentVec<usize, String>>::new();
        let n = 10;
        add_components(&mut vec, n);
        // Remove components in reverse order for fun.
        // Check if components have been removed properly.
        for i in (0..n).rev() {
            let str = format!("{i}");
            assert_eq!(vec.unset(i), Some(str));
            assert!(vec.get(i).is_none());
            assert!(vec.get_mut(i).is_none());
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn clear_works() {
        let mut vec = <ComponentVec<usize, String>>::new();
        let n = 10;
        add_components(&mut vec, n);
        // Clear component vec and check if components have been removed properly.
        vec.clear();
        for i in 0..n {
            assert!(vec.get(i).is_none());
            assert!(vec.get_mut(i).is_none());
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn debug_works() {
        let mut vec = <ComponentVec<usize, String>>::new();
        add_components(&mut vec, 4);
        {
            let debug_str = format!("{vec:?}");
            let expected_str = "\

            ";
            assert_eq!(debug_str, expected_str);
        }

            let expected_str = "\
                ComponentVec {\n    \
                    components: {\n        \

/// An arena allocator with a given key and entity type.
///
/// For performance reasons the arena cannot deallocate single entities.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Arena<Key, T> {
    /// The items stored in the arena.
    items: Vec<T>,
    /// Marker for the compiler to associate the `Key` type.
    marker: PhantomData<Key>,
}

/// [`Arena`] does not store `Key` therefore it is `Send` without its bound.
unsafe impl<Key, T> Send for Arena<Key, T> where T: Send {}

/// [`Arena`] does not store `Key` therefore it is `Sync` without its bound.
unsafe impl<Key, T> Sync for Arena<Key, T> where T: Sync {}

impl<Key, T> Default for Arena<Key, T> {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl<Key, T> PartialEq for Arena<Key, T>
where
    T: PartialEq,
{
    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        self.items.eq(&other.items)
    }
}

impl<Key, T> Eq for Arena<Key, T> where T: Eq {}

impl<Key, T> Arena<Key, T> {
    /// Creates a new empty entity [`Arena`].
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            items: Vec::new(),
            marker: PhantomData,
        }
    }

    /// Returns the allocated number of entities.
    #[inline]
    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.items.len()
    }

    /// Returns `true` if the arena has not yet allocated entities.
    #[inline]
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.len() == 0
    }

    /// Clears all entities from the arena.
    #[inline]
    pub fn clear(&mut self) {
        self.items.clear();
    }

    /// Returns an iterator over the shared reference of the arena entities.
    #[inline]
    pub fn iter(&self) -> Iter<'_, Key, T> {
        Iter {
            iter: self.items.iter().enumerate(),
            marker: PhantomData,
        }
    }

    /// Returns an iterator over the exclusive reference of the arena entities.
    #[inline]
    pub fn iter_mut(&mut self) -> IterMut<'_, Key, T> {
        IterMut {
            iter: self.items.iter_mut().enumerate(),
            marker: PhantomData,
        }
    }
}

impl<Key, T> Arena<Key, T>
where
    Key: ArenaKey,
{
    /// Returns the next entity index.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If there are no more valid keys left for allocation.
    fn next_key(&self) -> Result<Key, ArenaError> {
        Key::from_usize(self.items.len()).ok_or(ArenaError::NotEnoughKeys)
    }

    /// Allocates a new entity and returns its index.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If there are no more valid keys left for allocation.
    /// - If the system ran out of heap memory.
    #[inline]
    pub fn alloc(&mut self, entity: T) -> Result<Key, ArenaError> {
        let key = self.next_key()?;
        self.items
            .try_reserve(1)
            .map_err(|_| ArenaError::OutOfSystemMemory)?;
        self.items.push(entity);
        Ok(key)
    }

    /// Allocates `amount` default initialized entities and returns their keys.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If there are no more valid keys left for allocation.
    /// - If the system ran out of heap memory.
    #[inline]
    pub fn alloc_many(&mut self, amount: usize) -> Result<Range<Key>, ArenaError>
    where
        T: Default,
    {
        let start = self.next_key()?;
        self.items
            .try_reserve(amount)
            .map_err(|_| ArenaError::OutOfSystemMemory)?;
        self.items
            .extend(iter::repeat_with(T::default).take(amount));
        let end = self.next_key()?;
        Ok(Range { start, end })
    }

    /// Returns a shared reference to the entity at the given key if any.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If the `key` is out of bounds.
    #[inline]
    pub fn get(&self, key: Key) -> Result<&T, ArenaError> {

END STEP

//! This tests that a host function called from Wasm can instantiate Wasm modules and does not deadlock.

use std::{fmt, sync::Arc};
use wasmi::{AsContextMut, Caller, Engine, Linker, Module, Store};

#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum Data {
    Uninit,
    Init {
        linker: Arc<Linker<Data>>,
        module: Arc<Module>,
    },
}

#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub enum Error {
    Uninit,
    InstantiationFailed,
}

impl fmt::Display for Error {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Error::Uninit => write!(f, "error: uninit"),
            Error::InstantiationFailed => write!(f, "error: instantiation failed"),
        }
    }
}

impl core::error::Error for Error {}
impl wasmi::errors::HostError for Error {}

#[test]
fn test_instantiate_in_host_call() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let mut store = <Store<Data>>::new(&engine, Data::Uninit);
    let wasm = r#"
        (module
            (import "env" "instantiate" (func $instantiate))
            (func (export "run")
                (call $instantiate)
            )
        )
    "#;
    let module = Module::new(&engine, wasm).unwrap();
    let mut linker = <Linker<Data>>::new(&engine);
    linker
        .func_wrap(
            "env",
            "instantiate",
            |mut caller: Caller<Data>| -> Result<(), wasmi::Error> {
                let mut store = caller.as_context_mut();
                let Data::Init { linker, module } = store.data() else {
                    return Err(wasmi::Error::host(Error::Uninit));
                };
                let linker = linker.clone();
                let module = module.clone();
                let _instance = linker
                    .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
                    .map_err(|_| wasmi::Error::host(Error::InstantiationFailed))?;
                Ok(())
            },
        )
        .unwrap();
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let run = instance
        .get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&mut store, "run")
        .unwrap();
    *store.data_mut() = Data::Init {
        linker: Arc::new(linker),
        module: Arc::new(module),
    };
    run.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap();
}

END STEP

//! Wasm coredump generation.
//!
//! This module implements generation of [Wasm coredumps] upon Wasm traps.
//! Coredumps are only generated if this feature is enabled via
//! [`Config::generate_coredump`](crate::Config::generate_coredump).
//!
//! [Wasm coredumps]: https://github.com/WebAssembly/tool-conventions/blob/main/Coredump.md

use crate::{
    Global, InstanceEntity, Memory, Mutability, ValType,
    core::{CoreGlobal, CoreMemory},
    engine::{CompiledCoredumpFunc, CoredumpFuncMeta, Stack, VmState},
    store::StoreInner,
};
use alloc::{format, string::String, vec::Vec};

/// A stack frame captured for a Wasm coredump.
pub(crate) struct CapturedFrame {
    /// The pointer of the [`InstanceEntity`] of the frame.
    pub instance_ptr: usize,
    /// The Wasm function index of the frame within its module.
    pub func_idx: u32,
    /// The local values of the frame.
    pub locals: Vec<CoredumpValue>,
    /// The operand stack values of the frame.
    pub stack: Vec<CoredumpValue>,
}

/// A single value captured for a Wasm coredump.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub(crate) enum CoredumpValue {
    /// A value that could not be recovered.
    Missing,
    /// An `i32` value.
    I32(i32),
    /// An `i64` value.
    I64(i64),
    /// An `f32` value encoded as its IEEE 754 bits.
    F32(u32),
    /// An `f64` value encoded as its IEEE 754 bits.
    F64(u64),
}

/// A guard registering a [`Stack`] as currently active.
///
/// # Note
///
/// This is required to correctly generate Wasm coredumps for re-entrant
/// Wasm executions where multiple [`Stack`]s may be active at the same time.
pub(crate) struct ActiveStackGuard {
    stack: *mut Stack,
    store: *mut StoreInner,
}

impl ActiveStackGuard {
    /// Creates a new [`ActiveStackGuard`] for the given [`Stack`] and [`StoreInner`].
    pub(crate) fn new(stack: &mut Stack, store: &mut StoreInner) -> Self {
        let stack = stack as *mut Stack;
        let store = store as *mut StoreInner;
        // Safety: the store is alive for the entire duration of the guard.
        unsafe { (&mut *store).push_active_stack(stack) };
        Self { stack, store }
    }
}

impl Drop for ActiveStackGuard {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        // Safety: the store is alive for the entire duration of the guard.
        unsafe { (&mut *self.store).pop_active_stack(self.stack) };
    }
}

/// Generates a Wasm coredump for the currently active execution.
///
/// Returns `None` if coredump generation is not possible.
pub(crate) fn generate(state: &mut VmState<'_>) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
    let store = state.store.inner();
    let code = state.code;
    let executable_name = String::from(store.engine().config().get_coredump_executable_name());
    let funcs = code.collect_coredump_funcs();

    // Capture frames from all active stacks, innermost first.
    let stack_pointers = store.active_stacks();
    let mut frames = Vec::new();
    for &stack_addr in stack_pointers.iter().rev() {
        // Safety: the active stacks are alive for the entire duration of the
        // innermost execution and never mutated while we generate the coredump.
        let stack = unsafe { &*(stack_addr as *mut Stack) };
        let mut captured = capture_stack(stack, store, &funcs);
        frames.append(&mut captured);
    }

    // Collect modules, instances, memories and globals from the store.
    let mut modules: Vec<ModuleInfo> = Vec::new();
    let mut instances: Vec<&InstanceEntity> = Vec::new();
    let mut memories: Vec<*const CoreMemory> = Vec::new();
    let mut globals: Vec<*const CoreGlobal> = Vec::new();

    for instance in store.instances() {
        if !instance.is_initialized() {
            continue;
        }
        // Add module.
        if let Some(module) = instance.module() {
            let id = module.unique_id();
            if !modules.iter().any(|m| m.id == id) {
                let name = module
                    .name()
                    .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("module-{}", modules.len()));
                modules.push(ModuleInfo { id, name });
            }
        }
        // Add memories and globals.
        for memory in instance.memories() {
            let core = store.resolve_memory(memory);
            let ptr = core as *const CoreMemory;
            if !memories.contains(&ptr) {
                memories.push(ptr);
            }
        }
        for global in instance.globals() {
            let core = store.resolve_global(global);
            if !core.ty().content().is_num() {
                continue;
            }
            let ptr = core as *const CoreGlobal;
            if !globals.contains(&ptr) {
                globals.push(ptr);
            }
        }

END STEP

STEP 207 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && sed -n '130,300p' crates/wasmi/src/coredump.rs
OBSERVATION:
returncode=0
        }
        instances.push(instance);
    }

    // Build maps from entity pointer to coredump index.
    let module_index = |instance: &InstanceEntity| -> u32 {
        let Some(module) = instance.module() else {
            return 0;
        };
        let id = module.unique_id();
        modules.iter().position(|m| m.id == id).unwrap_or(0) as u32
    };
    let memory_index = |memory: &Memory| -> u32 {
        let core = store.resolve_memory(memory);
        let ptr = core as *const CoreMemory;
        memories
            .iter()
            .position(|m| *m == ptr)
            .unwrap_or(u32::MAX as usize) as u32
    };
    let global_index = |global: &Global| -> Option<u32> {
        let core = store.resolve_global(global);
        if !core.ty().content().is_num() {
            return None;
        }
        let ptr = core as *const CoreGlobal;
        Some(
            globals
                .iter()
                .position(|g| *g == ptr)
                .unwrap_or(u32::MAX as usize) as u32,
        )
    };
    let instance_index = |ptr: usize| -> u32 {
        instances
            .iter()
            .position(|i| (*i) as *const InstanceEntity as usize == ptr)
            .unwrap_or(u32::MAX as usize) as u32
    };

    let mut out = Vec::new();

    // 1. "core" custom section.
    let mut core_data = Vec::new();
    core_data.push(0x00);
    push_name(&mut core_data, &executable_name);
    write_custom_section(&mut out, "core", &core_data);

    // 2. Memory section.
    {
        let mut contents = Vec::new();
        push_uleb(&mut contents, memories.len() as u64);
        for &memory_ptr in &memories {
            // Safety: all memory pointers refer to live entities in the store.
            let memory = unsafe { &*memory_ptr };
            let ty = memory.dynamic_ty();
            let minimum = ty.minimum();
            let maximum = ty.maximum();
            let mut flags = 0u64;
            if maximum.is_some() {
                flags |= 0x01;
            }
            if ty.is_64() {
                flags |= 0x04;
            }
            push_uleb(&mut contents, flags);
            push_uleb(&mut contents, minimum);
            if let Some(maximum) = maximum {
                push_uleb(&mut contents, maximum);
            }
        }
        write_section(&mut out, 5, &contents);
    }

    // 3. Global section.
    {
        let mut contents = Vec::new();
        push_uleb(&mut contents, globals.len() as u64);
        for &global_ptr in &globals {
            // Safety: all global pointers refer to live entities in the store.
            let global = unsafe { &*global_ptr };
            let ty = global.ty();
            push_val_type(&mut contents, ty.content());
            contents.push(match ty.mutability() {
                Mutability::Const => 0x00,
                Mutability::Var => 0x01,
            });
            let value = global.get();
            write_const_expr(&mut contents, ty.content(), value.raw().to_bits64());
        }
        write_section(&mut out, 6, &contents);
    }

    // 4. Data section.
    {
        let mut contents = Vec::new();
        push_uleb(&mut contents, memories.len() as u64);
        for (idx, &memory_ptr) in memories.iter().enumerate() {
            // Safety: all memory pointers refer to live entities in the store.
            let memory = unsafe { &*memory_ptr };
            let data = memory.data();
            if idx == 0 {
                contents.push(0x00);
            } else {
                contents.push(0x02);
                push_uleb(&mut contents, idx as u64);
            }
            // i32.const offset 0, end
            contents.push(0x41);
            push_uleb(&mut contents, 0);
            contents.push(0x0B);
            push_uleb(&mut contents, data.len() as u64);
            contents.extend_from_slice(data);
        }
        write_section(&mut out, 11, &contents);
    }

    // 5. "coremodules" custom section.
    let mut modules_data = Vec::new();
    push_uleb(&mut modules_data, modules.len() as u64);
    for module in &modules {
        modules_data.push(0x00);
        push_name(&mut modules_data, &module.name);
    }
    write_custom_section(&mut out, "coremodules", &modules_data);

    // 6. "coreinstances" custom section.
    let mut instances_data = Vec::new();
    push_uleb(&mut instances_data, instances.len() as u64);
    for instance in &instances {
        instances_data.push(0x00);
        push_uleb(&mut instances_data, module_index(instance) as u64);
        let memories = instance
            .memories()
            .iter()
            .map(memory_index)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        push_uleb(&mut instances_data, memories.len() as u64);
        for memory in memories {
            push_uleb(&mut instances_data, memory as u64);
        }
        let globals = instance
            .globals()
            .iter()
            .filter_map(global_index)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        push_uleb(&mut instances_data, globals.len() as u64);
        for global in globals {
            push_uleb(&mut instances_data, global as u64);
        }
    }
    write_custom_section(&mut out, "coreinstances", &instances_data);

    // 7. "corestack" custom section.
    let mut stack_data = Vec::new();
    stack_data.push(0x00);
    push_name(&mut stack_data, "main");
    push_uleb(&mut stack_data, frames.len() as u64);
    for frame in &frames {
        stack_data.push(0x00);
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, instance_index(frame.instance_ptr) as u64);
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, frame.func_idx as u64);
        // Note: Wasmi does not retain enough information to recover the
        //       exact Wasm code offset of the frame.
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, 0);
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, frame.locals.len() as u64);
        for value in &frame.locals {
            write_value(&mut stack_data, *value);
        }
        push_uleb(&mut stack_data, frame.stack.len() as u64);
        for value in &frame.stack {

END STEP
